<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed <i>A</i> &gt; 0, a learner that retains at most $`Ad^2`$ bits between fresh exact Gaussian linear measurements needs $`\Omega_A(d\log(1/\epsilon))`$ measurements to estimate a uniformly random unit vector to angular error at most <i>ϵ</i>, for any $`0\lt \epsilon\le 1/10`$, with probability at least 2/3. The constant is absolute for $`o(d^2)`$ memory.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-main)): For every fixed finite $A>0$, there are constants $c_A>0$ and $d_A$ such that the following holds. Let $d\ge d_A$, let $0\le M\le Ad^2$ be an integer, and let $0<\epsilon\le1/10$. For any learner in Definition <a href="#h:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:model">1</a>, draw $S\sim\sigma_d$ independently of the rows and the learner’s data-independent randomness. If $$\mathbb P\{\arccos\langle \widehat s,S\rangle\le\epsilon\}\ge\frac23,$$ then its prescribed horizon satisfies $$T\ge c_A\,d\log(1/\epsilon).$$
  - Explanation: Finite retained memory imposes a precision-dependent sample lower bound for the uniform spherical prior.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-subquadratic)): Let $M(d)\ge0$ be integer-valued with $M(d)=o(d^2)$, and let $0<\epsilon(d)\le1/10$ be any accuracy sequence. A family of learners in Definition <a href="#h:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:model">1</a> with uniform-sphere average success at least $2/3$ satisfies $$T(d)\ge c\,d\log(1/\epsilon(d))$$ for all sufficiently large $d$, where $c>0$ is absolute. The dimension threshold may depend on the memory sequence.
  - Explanation: Subquadratic retained memory permits an absolute lower-bound constant, after a sequence-dependent dimension threshold.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-worst-signal)): The conclusions of Theorem <a href="#h:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:main">2</a> and Corollary <a href="#h:subquadratic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:subquadratic">3</a> also hold when their average-success assumption is replaced by $$\mathbb P\{\arccos\langle \widehat s,s\rangle\le\epsilon\}\ge\frac23 \qquad\text{for every }s\in S^{d-1},$$ where this probability is over the rows and the learner’s randomness.
  - Explanation: An every-signal success guarantee implies the same sample lower bound by averaging.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-local-caps)): For $d\ge2$, $z\in S^{d-1}$, and $r>0$, $$\label{h:local:cap} \sigma_d(B(z,r))\le r^{d-1}.$$ Consequently, if $Q$ is any probability law on $S^{d-1}$ independent of the signal and $$h(s)=Q\{v:\arccos\langle v,s\rangle\le\epsilon\},$$ then $h$ has local mass at scale $R=\epsilon$: $$\label{h:local:terminal} \int_{B(z,r)}h\,d\sigma_d\le(\epsilon r)^{(d-1)/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Spherical cap mass initializes the backward local-mass invariant for signal-independent terminal outputs.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-dv-finite-density)): Let $d,m\ge1$ and $p\ge2$ be integers, and let $\beta>m+p-2$, $H,R>0$, and $z\in\mathbb R^d$. Suppose that $\mu$ is a finite nonnegative Borel measure supported on $B(z,R)$ and satisfying $$\label{h:dv:ambient-bound} \mu(B(x,r))\le Hr^\beta \qquad(x\in\mathbb R^d,\ r>0).$$ For $0\le j\le p-2$, put $$J_j=1+3^j2^\beta\frac{m}{\beta-j-m},$$ and define $$\label{h:dv:moment-scale} \mathcal B=(2\pi)^{-m(p-1)/(2p)} H R^{\beta-m(1-1/p)} \left(\prod_{j=0}^{p-2}J_j\right)^{1/p}.$$ Then $\eta_\mu$ has a nonnegative jointly Borel density $F$ with respect to $\lambda_m$, which can be chosen to vanish outside $\mathcal E_{z,R}$, and $$\label{h:dv:finite-density-bound} \\|F\\|_{L^p(\lambda_m)}\le\mathcal B.$$
  - Explanation: Ambient ball growth controls the joint density of a Gaussian matrix and exact labels without normalizing the signal measure.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-dv-ambient-gram)): Under the assumptions of Theorem <a href="#h:dv:finite-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:dv:finite-density">6</a>, use the factors $J_j$ defined there. If $L$ is an affine $j$-plane with $0\le j\le p-2$, then, for $0<t\le R$, $$\begin{aligned} \mu\{s:\operatorname{dist}(s,L)\le t\} &\le 3^j2^\beta H R^j t^{\beta-j}, \label{h:dv:tube}\\ \int\operatorname{dist}(s,L)^{-m}\,d\mu(s) &\le J_j H R^{\beta-m}. \label{h:dv:inverse-distance} \end{aligned}$$ For a tuple $(s_1,\ldots,s_p)$, let $V=[s_2-s_1,\ldots,s_p-s_1]$. The matrix $V$ has full column rank for $\mu^{\otimes p}$-almost every tuple, and $$\int\det(V^{\mathsf T}V)^{-m/2}\,d\mu^{\otimes p} \le H^p R^{\beta p-m(p-1)} \prod_{j=0}^{p-2}J_j. \label{h:dv:gram-product}$$ The integrand in <a href="#h:dv:gram-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:dv:gram-product">[h:dv:gram-product]</a> may be assigned any value on the null set of rank-deficient tuples.
  - Explanation: Ambient growth bounds inverse affine distances and the inverse Gram determinant moment.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-proj-gaussian)): Let $d,m\ge1$ and $k\ge2$ be integers, let $\delta>0$, and let $t_1,\ldots,t_k\in\mathbb R^d$. Put $D=[t_2-t_1,\ldots,t_k-t_1]$. If $A$ has law $\gamma_m$, then $$\label{h:proj:gaussian-identity} \begin{split} \mathbb E_A\int_{\mathbb R^m}\prod_{i=1}^k\phi_\delta(y-At_i)\,dy ={}&(2\pi)^{-m(k-1)/2}\\ &{}\times \det\!\left(D^{\mathsf T}D+ \delta^2(I_{k-1}+\mathbf1\mathbf1^{\mathsf T})\right)^{-m/2}. \end{split}$$ If $D$ has full column rank, the right side is at most $(2\pi)^{-m(k-1)/2}\det(D^{\mathsf T}D)^{-m/2}$.
  - Explanation: A Gaussian difference covariance gives the exact regularized determinant identity, including dependent tuples.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-dv-gaussian-moment)): Under the assumptions of Theorem <a href="#h:dv:finite-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:dv:finite-density">6</a>, use the scale $\mathcal B$ from <a href="#h:dv:moment-scale" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:dv:moment-scale">[h:dv:moment-scale]</a>. Let $\phi_\delta$ be the $N(0,\delta^2I_m)$ density and put $$F_\delta(A,y)=\int\phi_\delta(y-As)\,d\mu(s), \qquad \delta>0.$$ Then $F_\delta$ is jointly Borel, and, with $V=[s_2-s_1,\ldots,s_p-s_1]$, $$\begin{aligned} \int F_\delta^p\,d\lambda_m &=(2\pi)^{-m(p-1)/2} \int\det\!\left( V^{\mathsf T}V+ \delta^2(I_{p-1}+\mathbf 1\mathbf 1^{\mathsf T}) \right)^{-m/2}\,d\mu^{\otimes p} \notag\\ &\le \mathcal B^p. \label{h:dv:gaussian-moment-bound} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The smoothed density moment retains the full mass factor and is bounded uniformly in the smoothing scale.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-proj-density-by-tests)): Let $E$ be a Euclidean space, let $\lambda$ be a locally finite, sigma-finite Borel measure on $E$, and let $\eta$ be a finite nonnegative Borel measure on $E$. Suppose $p>1$, $U\ge0$, and $$\left\|\int\psi\,d\eta\right\| \le U\\|\psi\\|_{L^{p/(p-1)}(\lambda)} \qquad\text{for every real }\psi\in C_c(E).$$ Then $\eta=f\lambda$ for a nonnegative $f\in L^p(\lambda)$ with $\\|f\\|_p\le U$.
  - Explanation: Compactly supported continuous tests identify a nonnegative density with the asserted moment norm.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-proj-normalized)): Use the parameters in <a href="#h:local:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:local:parameters">[h:local:parameters]</a>, and let $1\le m\le q$. Let $\nu$ be a finite nonnegative Borel measure of mass at most one, supported on the unit ball in $\mathbb R^d$, such that $$\label{h:proj:growth} \nu(B(t,\rho))\le\rho^a \qquad(t\in\operatorname{supp}\nu,\ \rho>0).$$ The image of $\gamma_m(dA)\nu(dt)$ under $(A,t)\mapsto(A,At)$ has a nonnegative jointly Borel density $f_\nu$ relative to $\lambda_m$, and $$\label{h:proj:norm} \\|f_\nu\\|_{L^q(\lambda_m)}\le\exp(Cd).$$ The constant is independent of $\nu$ and $m$.
  - Explanation: The normalized local-growth regime gives a uniform exponential-in-dimension density norm bound.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-ev-ellipsoid)): Let $1\le m\le d$, $z\in\mathbb R^d$, and $R>0$. Use the Borel set $\mathcal E_{z,R}$ from <a href="#h:dv:support-sets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:dv:support-sets">[h:dv:support-sets]</a>. There is an absolute $C_{\mathrm E}\ge1$ such that, for a standard Gaussian $m$-by-$d$ matrix, $$\begin{aligned} \lambda_m(\mathcal E_{z,R}) &=v_mR^m\mathbb E_A\sqrt{\det(AA^{\mathsf T})} \notag\\ &\le v_mR^m d^{m/2} \le R^m(2\pi e d/m)^{m/2} \le C_{\mathrm E}^dR^m . \label{h:ev:ellipsoid-volume} \end{aligned}$$ Every density of the exact image of a measure supported on $B(z,R)$ vanishes almost everywhere outside $\mathcal E_{z,R}$.
  - Explanation: The exact projected ellipsoid provides a common support with controlled average volume, including short blocks.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-block-bound)): Let $1\le m\le q$ and $N\ge1$. Suppose the measurable functions $h_1,\ldots,h_N:S^{d-1}\to[0,1]$ have local mass at a common scale $R>0$, as in <a href="#h:local:bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:local:bound">[h:local:bound]</a>. Let $g_j:\mathbb R^{m\times d}\times\mathbb R^m\to[0,1]$ be Borel routing probabilities with $\sum_{j=1}^N g_j\le1$, and define $$h(s)=\sum_{j=1}^N h_j(s)\int g_j(A,As)\,d\gamma_m(A).$$ Then, for every $z\in S^{d-1}$ and $r>0$, $$\label{h:block:mass} \int_{B(z,r)}h\,d\sigma \le\exp(Cd)N^{1/q}(Rr)^a.$$ Equivalently, $h$ has local mass at scale $$\label{h:block:scale} R'=R\exp(Cd/a)N^{1/(aq)}.$$
  - Explanation: The backward block estimate cancels the localization-radius loss and charges a root of the destination count.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-stream-categories)): For a fixed-seed Borel learner with the state sets, output laws, and phase convention just specified, and the Borel terminal score $\ell$, let $0\le t<T$, $u\in Q_t$, and $1\le m\le T-t$. There are an integer $$\label{h:stream:destinations} 1\le N\le(m+2)2^M,$$ Borel routing functions $g_j:\mathbb R^{m\times d}\times\mathbb R^m\to[0,1]$ with $\sum_jg_j\le1$, and Borel functions $h_j:S^{d-1}\to[0,1]$ such that, for every $s\in S^{d-1}$, $$\label{h:stream:category-representation} h_{t,u}(s)=\sum_{j=1}^N h_j(s) \int g_j(A,As)\,d\gamma_m(A).$$ Here $\gamma_m$ is the law of an $m$-by-$d$ standard Gaussian matrix. Each destination is either a terminal state $w\in Q_k$ at an index $k\in\{t,\ldots,t+m\}$, with score function $$h^{\mathrm{out}}_{k,w}(s) =\int_{S^{d-1}}\ell(s,v)\,\mathsf Q_{k,w}(dv),$$ or a continuing state $w\in Q_{t+m}$ before its stopping check, with score function $h_{t+m,w}(s)$. The terminal output law $\mathsf Q_{k,w}$ depends only on its state and index. The value $g_j(A,b)$ is the probability of destination $j$ when the block data are $(A,b)$.
  - Explanation: A block has terminal destinations indexed by local stopping offsets and a separate set of continuing destinations.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-stream-borel)): Let $d\ge2$, let $\pi$ be a Borel probability measure on $S^{d-1}$, and let $X\sim\gamma_1=N(0,I_d)$ be independent of $S\sim\pi$. Suppose that $$\label{h:stream:pair-domination} \mathcal L(X,\langle X,S\rangle)\ll \lambda,\qquad \lambda(dx,dy)=\gamma_1(dx)\,dy$$ on $\mathbb R^d\times\mathbb R$. Fix an integer horizon $T\ge0$ and finite state sets at every sample index. For each state and index, suppose every coordinate of the finite vector of transition and stopping probabilities is measurable in the $\lambda$-completion of the Borel sigma field. A terminal output law is a Borel probability measure on $S^{d-1}$ indexed only by its terminal state and stopping index. Decisions made without a new sample also depend only on the state and index.* *There are everywhere-defined Borel transition and stopping kernels, with the same state sets and terminal output laws, whose $\pi$-prior joint law of the signal, state path, stopping index and output is the same as for the original rules. The domination <a href="#h:stream:pair-domination" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:stream:pair-domination">[h:stream:pair-domination]</a> holds when $\pi=\sigma$ is uniform spherical probability, for every $d\ge2$.
  - Explanation: Dominated fresh-pair laws permit everywhere-defined Borel versions that preserve the experiment under its prior.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-stream-success)): There are absolute constants $C,d_0>0$ with the following property. For $d\ge d_0$, every learner in the stated finite-state model with $M\ge0$ bits and an integer deterministic horizon $T\ge0$ has, for $S\sim\sigma$ independent of its fresh Gaussian rows, $$\label{h:stream:success-bound} \mathbb P\{\arccos\langle\widehat s,S\rangle\le\epsilon\} \le \min\left\{1, \left[2\epsilon\exp\!\left( C\left(1+\frac{M}{d^2}\right) \left\lceil\frac{T}{q}\right\rceil\right)\right]^a \right\},$$ where $0<\epsilon\le1/10$ and $a=(d-1)/2$, $q=\lfloor(d-1)/8\rfloor$ as in <a href="#h:local:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:local:parameters">[h:local:parameters]</a>. The probability includes data-independent initialization, the shared seed, and fresh random choices.
  - Explanation: Backward induction bounds angular success at a deterministic finite horizon, including randomized initialization and shared seeds.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-ev-reflection)): Let $d\ge1$ be an integer and $T\ge0$. Let $S$ be uniform on $S^{d-1}$. Let a matrix $A$ with $d$ columns, independent of $S$, have row span $U$ of dimension at most $T$. Suppose an output $\widehat S$ is drawn from a probability kernel depending only on $(A,AS)$. Put $u=P_US$ and $v=(I-P_U)S$. For an arbitrary output in $\mathbb R^d$ and $0<\epsilon<1$, $$\label{h:ev:direct-residual} \Pr\{\\|\widehat S-S\\|\le\epsilon\} \le\frac12+\frac{T}{2d(1-\epsilon^2)}.$$ More generally, for $0<b_0<1$ and $0\le\epsilon<\sqrt{1-b_0}$, $$\label{h:ev:antipodal} \Pr\{\\|\widehat S-S\\|\le\epsilon\} \le\frac12+\frac12\Pr\{\\|u\\|^2\ge b_0\} \le\frac12+\frac{T}{2b_0d}.$$ For a unit output one also has $$\label{h:ev:reflection-expectation} \mathbb E\langle\widehat S,v\rangle=0,\qquad \mathbb E\langle\widehat S,S\rangle \le\mathbb E\\|u\\|\le\sqrt{T/d}.$$
  - Explanation: Reflection across the observed row span bounds full-data estimation success and correlation with the residual signal.
- **C17** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-alt-selection)): Let $(E,\mathcal E,\lambda)$ be a measure space, let $\mathcal D\in\mathcal E$ have finite measure, let $p>1$, let $U\ge0$, and let $N\ge1$ be an integer. Suppose finite nonnegative measures $\eta_1,\ldots,\eta_N$ are supported on $\mathcal D$, and that for every measurable $0\le g\le1$, $$\label{h:alt:test} \int g\,d\eta_j \le U\left(\int_{\mathcal D}g\,d\lambda\right)^{1-1/p} \qquad(1\le j\le N).$$ For measurable routes $g_j\ge0$ with $\sum_jg_j\le1$, $$\label{h:alt:selection-bound} \sum_{j=1}^N\int g_j\,d\eta_j \le N^{1/p}U\,\lambda(\mathcal D)^{1-1/p}.$$ A sufficient condition for <a href="#h:alt:test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:alt:test">[h:alt:test]</a> is $\eta_j=f_j\lambda$, where $f_j\ge0$ vanishes outside $\mathcal D$ and $\\|f_j\\|_{L^p(\lambda)}\le U$.* *In particular, if $r>0$, $m,\beta\in\mathbb R$, and positive constants $H,V,L$ satisfy $$U\le HLr^{\beta-m(1-1/p)},\qquad \lambda(\mathcal D)\le Vr^m,$$ then the right side of <a href="#h:alt:selection-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:alt:selection-bound">[h:alt:selection-bound]</a> is at most $$\label{h:alt:scale-cancellation} N^{1/p}H V^{1-1/p}Lr^\beta.$$
  - Explanation: Selection among measures on a common finite-measure domain costs a root of the number of destinations.
- **C18** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-cube-density)): Let $k$ be an integer with $1\le k\le p$, and let a Borel function $f:Q\to[0,1]$ satisfy <a href="#h:cube:local" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:cube:local">[h:cube:local]</a> with $L\ge0$. For $z\in\mathbb R^n$ and $r>0$, define the finite joint measure $$\label{h:cube:joint-measure} \eta_{f,z,r}(E) =\int\!\int_{\mathcal B(z,r)} \mathbf 1_E(A,A\iota(u))f(u)\,du\,\gamma_k(dA)$$ for Borel $E\subseteq\mathbb R^{k\times d}\times\mathbb R^k$. There is a universal $C_2\ge1$ such that $\eta_{f,z,r}=h_{f,z,r}\lambda_k$ for a nonnegative density with $$\label{h:cube:density-bound} \\|h_{f,z,r}\\|_{L^p(\lambda_k)} \le C_2^n Lr^{\beta-k(1-1/p)}.$$ If $\mathcal B(z,r)\ne\varnothing$, fix any $u_*\in\mathcal B(z,r)$ and set $$\label{h:cube:ellipsoid-support} \mathcal E_{u_*,r} =\{(A,y):y\in A\iota(u_*)+A B_d(0,4r)\}.$$ Every such density, for this same $z,r,u_*$, vanishes almost everywhere outside $\mathcal E_{u_*,r}$, and $$\label{h:cube:ellipsoid-volume} \lambda_k(\mathcal E_{u_*,r})\le C_2^n r^k.$$
  - Explanation: Local cube mass transfers to a joint exact-label density on a common projected ellipsoid.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-cube-block)): Let $k,N$ be integers with $1\le k\le p$ and $N\ge1$, and let Borel $f_1,\ldots,f_N:Q\to[0,1]$ satisfy <a href="#h:cube:local" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:cube:local">[h:cube:local]</a> with the same $L\ge0$. Suppose Borel functions $g_v:\mathbb R^{k\times d}\times\mathbb R^k\to[0,\infty)$ obey $\sum_{v=1}^N g_v(A,y)\le1$. Then $$F(u)=\mathbb E_{A\sim\gamma_k} \sum_{v=1}^N g_v(A,A\iota(u))f_v(u)$$ satisfies <a href="#h:cube:local" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:cube:local">[h:cube:local]</a> with $C_3^nN^{1/p}L$ in place of $L$, for a universal $C_3\ge1$.
  - Explanation: For the paired-coordinate prior, the common support cancels the radius loss during selection.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-cube-success)): There are universal constants $D\ge e$, $d_0\ge16$, and $c>0$ with the following property. Let $d\ge d_0$, $M\ge0$, and $T\ge0$ be integers, with $M\le d^2$ and $0<\epsilon\le1/10$. Every learner in Definition <a href="#h:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:model">1</a> with these memory and sample bounds has angular success probability under $s=\iota(u)$, $u\sim du$, at most $$\label{h:cube:success-bound} \min\left\{1,\, D^{n(m+1)}\epsilon^{n/4}\right\}, \qquad m=\left\lceil\frac{T}{k_0}\right\rceil,\qquad k_0=\left\lfloor\frac n4\right\rfloor.$$ The probability averages over $u$, the fresh Gaussian samples, and the learner’s randomness. If that same learner has angular success probability at least $2/3$ for every $s\in S^{d-1}$, then $$\label{h:cube:sample-result} T\ge c\,d\log(1/\epsilon).$$
  - Explanation: The cube prior yields a finite precision bound; the full accuracy-range lower bound uses an every-signal premise.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-cube-inverse)): Let $f:Q\to[0,1]$ be Borel and satisfy <a href="#h:cube:local" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:cube:local">[h:cube:local]</a> with $L\ge0$. Let $k$ be an integer with $1\le k\le p$, and let $V\subseteq\mathbb R^n$ be a nonempty affine subspace of dimension $j\le p-2$. There is a universal $C_1\ge1$ such that, for every $z\in\mathbb R^n$, $r>0$, and $0<t\le1$, $$\begin{aligned} \int_{\mathcal B(z,r)}f(u) \mathbf1_{\{\operatorname{dist}(u,V)\le\sqrt n\,tr\}}\,du &\le C_1^n Lr^\beta t^{\beta-j}, \label{h:cube:tube}\\ \int_{\mathcal B(z,r)}f(u) \left(\frac{\operatorname{dist}(u,V)}{\sqrt n}\right)^{-k}\,du &\le C_1^n Lr^{\beta-k}. \label{h:cube:inverse-bound} \end{aligned}$$ The second integrand is nonnegative and extended-valued on $V$.
  - Explanation: The local cube bound controls affine tubes and inverse-distance moments in parameter space.
- **C22** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-dv-measurable-test)): Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href="#h:dv:ambient-gram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:dv:ambient-gram">7</a>, the exact image measure satisfies $$\eta_\mu(E)\le \mathcal B\,\lambda_m(E)^{1-1/p} \label{h:dv:set-bound}$$ for every Borel set $E$ of finite $\lambda_m$-measure. In particular $\eta_\mu\ll\lambda_m$, and the same inequality holds on the $\lambda_m$-completion after extending $\eta_\mu$ to that completion. For every $\lambda_m$-completed-measurable $0\le\varphi\le1$, $$\int\varphi\,d\eta_\mu \le\mathcal B \left(\int_{\mathcal E_{z,R}}\varphi\,d\lambda_m\right)^{1-1/p}. \label{h:dv:test-bound}$$ An infinite integral on the right is allowed.
  - Explanation: A difference-kernel argument directly controls completed-measurable tests on exact observations.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-dv-gaussian-ac)): Let $d\ge2$ and $1\le m<d$. If $A:\mathbb R^d\to\mathbb R^m$ has full row rank, then $A_\#\sigma_d$ is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure. The same holds for $A_\#\mu$ whenever $\mu$ is a finite Borel measure with $\mu\ll\sigma_d$.
  - Explanation: Every full-row-rank projection of spherical area, or a measure dominated by it, is absolutely continuous.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-dv-joint-density)): Let $d\ge2$, $1\le m<d$, and let $\mu$ be a finite nonnegative Borel measure with $\mu\ll\sigma_d$. Then $\eta_\mu$ has a nonnegative jointly Borel density $F(A,y)$ with respect to $\lambda_m$. For $\gamma_m$-almost every $A$, $F(A,\cdot)$ is a density of $A_\#\mu$.* *If $\mu$ is supported on $B(z,R)$, the density can be chosen to vanish outside the Borel set $\mathcal E_{z,R}\subseteq\mathcal S_{z,R}$ defined in <a href="#h:dv:support-sets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:dv:support-sets">[h:dv:support-sets]</a>.
  - Explanation: A jointly Borel density identifies projection densities for almost every Gaussian matrix.
- **C25** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-dv-joint-l1)): In addition to the assumptions of Proposition <a href="#h:dv:ambient-gram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:dv:ambient-gram">7</a>, suppose that $d\ge2$, $1\le m<d$, and $\mu\ll\sigma_d$. Let $F$ be the joint density from Lemma <a href="#h:dv:joint-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:dv:joint-density">25</a>, and let $F_\delta$ be the Gaussian regularization in Lemma <a href="#h:dv:gaussian-moment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:dv:gaussian-moment">9</a>. Then $$\\|F_\delta-F\\|_{L^1(\lambda_m)}\longrightarrow0 \quad\hbox{as }\delta\downarrow0, \qquad \\|F\\|_{L^p(\lambda_m)}\le\mathcal B. \label{h:dv:joint-l1-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Gaussian regularizations converge in joint integral norm under the additional spherical absolute-continuity hypotheses.
- **C26** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-dv-pointwise-density)): Assume the same hypotheses as in Proposition <a href="#h:dv:joint-l1" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:dv:joint-l1">26</a>. For the Gaussian density $\phi_\delta$, define $$w_\delta(r) =v_m r^m\frac{r}{\delta^2}\phi_\delta(r e_1), \qquad r>0, \label{h:dv:radial-mixture}$$ where $e_1$ is a unit coordinate vector. This is a probability density on $(0,\infty)$, and $\phi_\delta$ is the corresponding mixture of uniform ball densities. Define $$F^\sharp(A,y)= \begin{cases} \displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}F_{1/i}(A,y), &\text{if this limit exists and is finite},\\ 0, &\text{otherwise}. \end{cases} \label{h:dv:pointwise-version}$$ Then $F^\sharp$ is jointly Borel. For every full-row-rank $A$, $F^\sharp(A,\cdot)$ is a density of $A_\#\mu$ up to a Lebesgue null set, and it is a density of $\eta_\mu$ with respect to $\lambda_m$. Moreover $$\int(F^\sharp)^p\,d\lambda_m\le\mathcal B^p. \label{h:dv:pointwise-moment}$$
  - Explanation: A specified Borel limit gives a density fiber for every full-row-rank matrix, outside a matrix-dependent null set of labels.
- **C27** (bound, [source](#stmt-auto-corollary-1)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#h:dv:ambient-gram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:dv:ambient-gram">7</a>, suppose that $$d\ge16,\qquad p=\lfloor d/8\rfloor,\qquad 1\le m\le p,\qquad \frac{d-1}{2}\le\beta\le\frac d2, \label{h:dv:parameters}$$ Then, for every $0\le j\le p-2$, $$\beta-j-m\ge\frac{d-1}{2}-2p+2 \ge\frac d4+\frac32, \qquad J_j\le C^d, \label{h:dv:dimension-margin}$$ where $C\ge1$ is an absolute constant. Thus the right side of <a href="#h:dv:gram-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:dv:gram-product">[h:dv:gram-product]</a> is at most $C^{d(p-1)}H^pR^{\beta p-m(p-1)}$, uniformly also for short blocks $m<p$.
  - Explanation: The dimension-scale regime keeps the inverse-distance denominators uniformly positive.
- **C28** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-ev-density)): Assume <a href="#h:dv:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:dv:parameters">[h:dv:parameters]</a>, and let $H,R>0$ and $z\in\mathbb R^d$. Let $f:S^{d-1}\to[0,1]$ be Borel measurable, let $\nu=f\sigma$, and suppose $\nu(B(x,r))\le H r^\beta$ for every $x\in\mathbb R^d$ and $r>0$. For $\mu=\mathbf 1_{B(z,R)}\nu$, the pushforward $A_\#\mu$ has, for almost every $A$, a Lebesgue density $F(A,\cdot)$. There is an absolute $C_{\mathrm D}\ge1$ for which a jointly measurable nonnegative choice of these densities satisfies $$\label{h:ev:density-moment} \int F(A,y)^p\,d\lambda_m(A,y) \le C_{\mathrm D}^{d(p-1)} H^pR^{\beta p-m(p-1)}.$$ The density represents the exact labels $As$.
  - Explanation: Spherical restrictions inherit the exact projection moment estimate from ambient ball growth.
- **C29** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-proj-volume)): For a standard Gaussian $m$-by-$d$ matrix $A$, where $1\le m\le d$, $$\label{h:proj:volume-bound} \mathbb E\,\operatorname{vol}_m B(0,\\|A\\|_{\mathrm{op}})\le\exp(Cd).$$
  - Explanation: Gaussian operator-norm moments control the average volume of an enclosing label ball.
- **C30** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-dv-hemisphere-cap)): Let $d\ge2$, $n=d-1$, and let $\sigma_d$ be uniform probability on $S^{d-1}$. Then $$\sigma_d(B(z,r))\le(4r)^n \qquad(z\in\mathbb R^d,\ r>0). \label{h:dv:hemisphere-ball}$$ Let $\epsilon>0$, let $\kappa$ be any probability law on $S^{d-1}$, independent of the target, and put $h(s)=\int\mathbf 1_{\{\\|s-u\\|\le\epsilon\}}\,\kappa(du)$. For $0<\beta<n$, every $z\in\mathbb R^d$, and every $r>0$, $$\int_{B(z,r)}h\,d\sigma_d \le4^n\min\{r^n,\epsilon^n\} \le4^n\epsilon^{n-\beta}r^\beta. \label{h:dv:terminal-coefficient}$$ The same upper bound holds for the angular-success function, since angular distance at most $\epsilon$ implies chordal distance at most $\epsilon$.
  - Explanation: A hemisphere argument gives an ambient spherical-ball estimate and terminal coefficient.
- **C31** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-ev-polar-cap)): Let $d\ge2$, $n=d-1$, and let $\sigma$ be uniform on $S^{d-1}$. There is an absolute $C_0\ge1$ such that $\sigma(B(z,R))\le C_0^dR^n$ for every ambient center $z$ and every $R>0$. Let $\epsilon>0$. If a unit output is drawn from a law independent of the signal, its success function $h(s)$, for either angular error at most $\epsilon$ or chordal error at most $\epsilon$, satisfies $$\label{h:ev:polar-terminal} \int_{B(z,R)}h\,d\sigma \le C_0^d\min(R^n,\epsilon^n) \le C_0^d\epsilon^{n-\beta}R^\beta \qquad(0<\beta<n).$$
  - Explanation: Polar cap estimates supply an alternative terminal coefficient for angular or chordal success.
- **C32** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-ev-coefficient-step)): Assume <a href="#h:dv:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:dv:parameters">[h:dv:parameters]</a>. Let $H>0$ and let $N\ge1$ be an integer. Let $h_1,\ldots,h_N:S^{d-1}\to[0,1]$ be Borel functions satisfying $$\int_{B(x,r)}h_w\,d\sigma\le H r^\beta \qquad(x\in\mathbb R^d,\ r>0,\ 1\le w\le N).$$ Let $g_w(A,y)\ge0$ be Borel functions with $\sum_wg_w\le1$, and define $$h(s)=\mathbb E_A\sum_{w=1}^N g_w(A,As)h_w(s).$$ There is an absolute $C_{\mathrm{step}}\ge1$ such that $$\label{h:ev:coefficient-bound} \int_{B(z,R)}h\,d\sigma \le C_{\mathrm{step}}^dN^{1/p}H R^\beta \qquad(z\in\mathbb R^d,\ R>0).$$
  - Explanation: One backward coefficient recurrence works for variable local exponents and actual short blocks.
- **C33** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-ev-coefficient-success)): Assume $d\ge16$, set $n=d-1$, $p=\lfloor d/8\rfloor$, and let $n/2\le\beta\le d/2$. Let $M,T\ge0$ be integers, $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, and consider a learner in Definition <a href="#h:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:model">1</a>. Under the uniform-sphere signal prior, write $P_{\mathrm{succ}}$ for its success probability with either angular or chordal error at most $\epsilon$, and put $b=\lceil T/p\rceil$. Then $$\label{h:ev:common-success} P_{\mathrm{succ}}\le \min\left\{1,\, 4^n\epsilon^{n-\beta} \left[C_{\mathrm{step}}^d \bigl((p+2)2^M\bigr)^{1/p}\right]^b\right\}.$$ The same assertion holds with the initial factor $4^n$ replaced by the polar-cap constant $C_0^d$.
  - Explanation: Iteration of the coefficient step yields a common finite success bound for angular or chordal error.
- **C34** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-ev-success)): Assume $d\ge16$ and $p=\lfloor d/8\rfloor$. Let $M,T\ge0$ be integers, let $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, and consider a learner in Definition <a href="#h:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="h:model">1</a> with these parameters. Draw $S\sim\sigma$ independently of its Gaussian rows $x_1,\ldots,x_T$ and its data-independent randomness, and set the exact labels $y_t=\langle x_t,S\rangle$. Write $\Pr_\sigma$ for the resulting joint law and put $b=\lceil T/p\rceil$. With an absolute $C_{\mathrm{step}}\ge1$, the uniform-prior angular success probability satisfies $$\label{h:ev:half-sphere-success} \Pr_\sigma\{\operatorname{angle}(\widehat S,S)\le\epsilon\} \le \min\!\left\{1,\, 4^d\epsilon^{(d-1)/2} \left[C_{\mathrm{step}}^d \bigl((p+2)2^M\bigr)^{1/p}\right]^b \right\}.$$ The uniform-prior chordal success probability satisfies $$\label{h:ev:half-ambient-success} \Pr_\sigma\{\\|\widehat S-S\\|\le\epsilon\} \le \min\!\left\{1,\, C_0^d\epsilon^{d/2-1} \left[C_{\mathrm{step}}^d \bigl((2p+4)2^M\bigr)^{1/p}\right]^b \right\}.$$ The second event contains the angular success event.
  - Explanation: The endpoint choices of the local exponent give distinct angular and chordal finite success bounds.
- **C35** (bound, [source](#stmt-h-end-linear)): For all sufficiently large $d$, let $0\le T\le d/4$ be an integer. Let $S$ be uniform on $S^{d-1}$, let $X$ be an independent $T$-by-$d$ standard Gaussian matrix, and put $Y=XS$. For every $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, any possibly randomized estimator $W\in\mathbb R^d$ based on the entire pair $(X,Y)$ satisfies $$\label{h:end:full-data-bound} \mathbb P\{\\|W-S\\|\le\epsilon\} \le \frac13+\left(\frac25\right)^{3d/4-1} <\frac23.$$ Consequently, a learner with uniform-sphere angular success at least $2/3$ at accuracy $\epsilon\le1/10$ must have $T>d/4$, even with unlimited memory and access to all pre-generated samples.
  - Explanation: Even an estimator retaining the full data needs a linear sample horizon at the stated constant accuracy.

      "exact_statement": "Under the assumptions of Theorem <a href=\"#h:dv:finite-density\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:finite-density\">6</a>, use the factors $J_j$ defined there. If $L$ is an affine $j$-plane with $0\\le j\\le p-2$, then, for $0<t\\le R$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mu\\{s:\\operatorname{dist}(s,L)\\le t\\}\n &\\le 3^j2^\\beta H R^j t^{\\beta-j},\n \\label{h:dv:tube}\\\\\n \\int\\operatorname{dist}(s,L)^{-m}\\,d\\mu(s)\n &\\le J_j H R^{\\beta-m}.\n \\label{h:dv:inverse-distance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For a tuple $(s_1,\\ldots,s_p)$, let $V=[s_2-s_1,\\ldots,s_p-s_1]$. The matrix $V$ has full column rank for $\\mu^{\\otimes p}$-almost every tuple, and $$\\int\\det(V^{\\mathsf T}V)^{-m/2}\\,d\\mu^{\\otimes p}\n \\le\n H^p R^{\\beta p-m(p-1)}\n \\prod_{j=0}^{p-2}J_j.\n \\label{h:dv:gram-product}$$ The integrand in <a href=\"#h:dv:gram-product\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:gram-product\">[h:dv:gram-product]</a> may be assigned any value on the null set of rank-deficient tuples.",
      "explanation": "Ambient growth bounds inverse affine distances and the inverse Gram determinant moment.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-ambient-gram",
          "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Theorem <a href=\"#h:dv:finite-density\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:finite-density\">6</a>, use the factors $J_j$ defined there.",

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-h-proj-gaussian",
        "exact_quote": "If $A$ has law $\\gamma_m$, then $$\\label{h:proj:gaussian-identity}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathbb E_A\\int_{\\mathbb R^m}\\prod_{i=1}^k\\phi_\\delta(y-At_i)\\,dy\n ={}&(2\\pi)^{-m(k-1)/2}\\\\\n &{}\\times\n \\det\\!\\left(D^{\\mathsf T}D+\n \\delta^2(I_{k-1}+\\mathbf1\\mathbf1^{\\mathsf T})\\right)^{-m/2}.\n\\end{split}$$ If $D$ has full column rank, the right side is at most $(2\\pi)^{-m(k-1)/2}\\det(D^{\\mathsf T}D)^{-m/2}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $d,m\\ge1$ and $k\\ge2$ be integers, let $\\delta>0$, and let $t_1,\\ldots,t_k\\in\\mathbb R^d$. Put $D=[t_2-t_1,\\ldots,t_k-t_1]$.         "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Theorem <a href=\"#h:dv:finite-density\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:finite-density\">6</a>, use the scale $\\mathcal B$ from <a href=\"#h:dv:moment-scale\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:moment-scale\">[h:dv:moment-scale]</a>. Let $\\phi_\\delta$ be the $N(0,\\delta^2I_m)$ density and put $$F_\\delta(A,y)=\\int\\phi_\\delta(y-As)\\,d\\mu(s),\n \\qquad \\delta>0.$$ Then $F_\\delta$ is jointly Borel, and, with $V=[s_2-s_1,\\ldots,s_p-s_1]$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int F_\\delta^p\\,d\\lambda_m\n &=(2\\pi)^{-m(p-1)/2}\n   \\int\\det\\!\\left(\n     V^{\\mathsf T}V+\n     \\delta^2(I_{p-1}+\\mathbf 1\\mathbf 1^{\\mathsf T})\n   \\right)^{-m/2}\\,d\\mu^{\\otimes p}\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\le \\mathcal B^p.\n \\label{h:dv:gaussian-moment-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-gaussian-moment",
          "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Theorem <a href=\"#h:dv:finite-density\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:finite-density\">6</a>, use the scale $\\mathcal B$ from <a href=\"#h:dv:moment-scale\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:moment-scale\">[h:dv:moment-scale]</a>.           "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-h-proj-density-by-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a Euclidean space, let $\\lambda$ be a locally finite, sigma-finite Borel measure on $E$, and let $\\eta$ be a finite nonnegative Borel measure on $E$. Suppose $p>1$, $U\\ge0$, and $$\\left|\\int\\psi\\,d\\eta\\right|\n \\le U\\|\\psi\\|_{L^{p/(p-1)}(\\lambda)}\n \\qquad\\text{for every real }\\psi\\in C_c(E).$$ Then $\\eta=f\\lambda$ for a nonnegative $f\\in L^p(\\lambda)$ with $\\|f\\|_p\\le U$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-proj-density-by-tests",

          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-ev-ellipsoid",
        "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le m\\le d$, $z\\in\\mathbb R^d$, and $R>0$. Use the Borel set $\\mathcal E_{z,R}$ from <a href=\"#h:dv:support-sets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:support-sets\">[h:dv:support-sets]</a>. There is an absolute $C_{\\mathrm E}\\ge1$ such that, for a standard Gaussian $m$-by-$d$ matrix, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lambda_m(\\mathcal E_{z,R})\n &=v_mR^m\\mathbb E_A\\sqrt{\\det(AA^{\\mathsf T})}\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\le v_mR^m d^{m/2}\n \\le R^m(2\\pi e d/m)^{m/2}\n \\le C_{\\mathrm E}^dR^m .\n \\label{h:ev:ellipsoid-volume}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Every density of the exact image of a measure supported on $B(z,R)$ vanishes almost everywhere outside $\\mathcal E_{z,R}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-ev-ellipsoid",

          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $1\\le m\\le d$, $z\\in\\mathbb R^d$, and $R>0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-block-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Then, for every $z\\in S^{d-1}$ and $r>0$, $$\\label{h:block:mass}\n \\int_{B(z,r)}h\\,d\\sigma\n \\le\\exp(Cd)N^{1/q}(Rr)^a.$$ Equivalently, $h$ has local mass at scale $$\\label{h:block:scale}\n R'=R\\exp(Cd/a)N^{1/(aq)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-block-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le m\\le q$ and $N\\ge1$. Suppose the measurable functions $h_1,\\ldots,h_N:S^{d-1}\\to[0,1]$ have local mass at a common scale $R>0$, as in <a href=\"#h:local:bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:local:bound\">[h:local:bound]</a>. Let $g_j:\\mathbb R^{m\\times d}\\times\\mathbb R^m\\to[0,1]$ be Borel routing probabilities with $\\sum_{j=1}^N g_j\\le1$, and define $$h(s)=\\sum_{j=1}^N h_j(s)\\int g_j(A,As)\\,d\\gamma_m(A).$$ Then, for every $z\\in S^{d-1}$ and $r>0$, $$\\label{h:block:mass}\n \\int_{B(z,r)}h\\,d\\sigma\n \\le\\exp(Cd)N^{1/q}(Rr)^a.$$ Equivalently, $h$ has local mass at scale $$\\label{h:block:scale}\n R'=R\\exp(Cd/a)N^{1/(aq)}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $1\\le m\\le q$ and $N\\ge1$. Suppose the measurable functions $h_1,\\ldots,h_N:S^{d-1}\\to[0,1]$ have local mass at a common scale $R>0$, as in <a href=\"#h:local:bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:local:bound\">[h:local:bound]</a>. Let $g_j:\\mathbb R^{m\\times d}\\times\\mathbb R^m\\to[0,1]$ be Borel routing probabilities with $\\sum_{j=1}^N g_j\\le1$, and define $$h(s)=\\sum_{j=1}^N h_j(s)\\int g_j(A,As)\\,d\\gamma_m(A).$$ Then, for every $z\\in S^{d-1}$ and $r>0$, $$\\label{h:block:mass}\n \\int_{B(z,r)}h\\,d\\sigma\n \\le\\exp(Cd)N^{1/q}(Rr)^a.$$ Equivalently, $h$ has local mass at scale $$\\label{h:block:scale}\n R'=R\\exp(Cd/a)N^{1/(aq)}.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-stream-categories",
        "exact_quote": "$$\\label{h:stream:destinations}\n 1\\le N\\le(m+2)2^M,$$ Borel routing functions $g_j:\\mathbb R^{m\\times d}\\times\\mathbb R^m\\to[0,1]$ with $\\sum_jg_j\\le1$, and Borel functions $h_j:S^{d-1}\\to[0,1]$ such that, for every $s\\in S^{d-1}$, $$\\label{h:stream:category-representation}\n h_{t,u}(s)=\\sum_{j=1}^N h_j(s)\n       \\int g_j(A,As)\\,d\\gamma_m(A).$$ Here $\\gamma_m$ is the law of an $m$-by-$d$ standard Gaussian matrix. Each destination is either a terminal state $w\\in Q_k$ at an index $k\\in\\{t,\\ldots,t+m\\}$, with score function $$h^{\\mathrm{out}}_{k,w}(s)\n =\\int_{S^{d-1}}\\ell(s,v)\\,\\mathsf Q_{k,w}(dv),$$ or a continuing state $w\\in Q_{t+m}$ before its stopping check, with score function $h_{t+m,w}(s)$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-stream-borel",
        "exact_quote": "*There are everywhere-defined Borel transition and stopping kernels, with the same state sets and terminal output laws, whose $\\pi$-prior joint law of the signal, state path, stopping index and output is the same as for the original rules. The domination <a href=\"#h:stream:pair-domination\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:stream:pair-domination\">[h:stream:pair-domination]</a> holds when $\\pi=\\sigma$ is uniform spherical probability, for every $d\\ge2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-stream-borel",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$, let $\\pi$ be a Borel probability measure on $S^{d-1}$, and let $X\\sim\\gamma_1=N(0,I_d)$ be independent of $S\\sim\\pi$. Suppose that $$\\label{h:stream:pair-domination}\n \\mathcal L(X,\\langle X,S\\rangle)\\ll\n \\lambda,\\qquad \\lambda(dx,dy)=\\gamma_1(dx)\\,dy$$ on $\\mathbb R^d\\times\\mathbb R$. Fix an integer horizon $T\\ge0$ and finite state sets at every sample index. For each state and index, suppose every coordinate of the finite vector of transition and stopping probabilities is measurable in the $\\lambda$-completion of the Borel sigma field. A terminal output law is a Borel probability measure on $S^{d-1}$ indexed only by its terminal state and stopping index. Decisions made without a new sample also depend only on the state and index.*\n\n*There are everywhere-defined Borel transition and stopping kernels, with the same state sets and terminal output laws, whose $\\pi$-prior joint law of the signal, state path, stopping index and output is the same as for the original rules. The domination <a href=\"#h:stream:pair-domination\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:stream:pair-domination\">[h:stream:pair-domination]</a> holds when $\\pi=\\sigma$ is uniform spherical probability, for every $d\\ge2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $d\\ge2$, let $\\pi$ be a Borel probability measure on $S^{d-1}$, and let $X\\sim\\gamma_1=N(0,I_d)$ be independent of $S\\sim\\pi$. Fix an integer horizon $T\\ge0$ and finite state sets at every sample index. For each state and index, suppose every coordinate of the finite vector of transition and stopping probabilities is measurable in the $\\lambda$-completion of the Borel sigma field. A terminal output law is a Borel probability measure on $S^{d-1}$ indexed only by its terminal state and stopping index. Decisions made without a new sample also depend only on the state and index.*\n\n*There are everywhere-defined Borel transition and stopping kernels, with the same state sets and terminal output laws, whose $\\pi$-prior joint law of the signal, state path, stopping index and output is the same as for the original rules. The domination <a href=\"#h:stream:pair-domination\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:stream:pair-domination\">[h:stream:pair-domination]</a> holds when $\\pi=\\sigma$ is uniform spherical probability, for every $d\\ge2$.",
      "explanation": "Dominated fresh-pair laws permit everywhere-defined Borel versions that preserve the experiment under its prior.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-h-stream-borel",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$, let $\\pi$ be a Borel probability measure on $S^{d-1}$, and let $X\\sim\\gamma_1=N(0,I_d)$ be independent of $S\\sim\\pi$. Fix an integer horizon $T\\ge0$ and finite state sets at every sample index.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-stream-success",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C,d_0>0$ with the following property. For $d\\ge d_0$, every learner in the stated finite-state model with $M\\ge0$ bits and an integer deterministic horizon $T\\ge0$ has, for $S\\sim\\sigma$ independent of its fresh Gaussian rows, $$\\label{h:stream:success-bound}\n \\mathbb P\\{\\arccos\\langle\\widehat s,S\\rangle\\le\\epsilon\\}\n \\le\n \\min\\left\\{1,\n \\left[2\\epsilon\\exp\\!\\left(\n C\\left(1+\\frac{M}{d^2}\\right)\n \\left\\lceil\\frac{T}{q}\\right\\rceil\\right)\\right]^a\n \\right\\},$$ where $0<\\epsilon\\le1/10$ and $a=(d-1)/2$, $q=\\lfloor(d-1)/8\\rfloor$ as in <a href=\"#h:local:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:local:parameters\">[h:local:parameters]</a>. The probability includes data-independent initialization, the shared seed, and fresh random choices.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-stream-success",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C,d_0>0$ with the following property.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-ev-reflection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge1$ be an integer and $T\\ge0$. Let $S$ be uniform on $S^{d-1}$. Let a matrix $A$ with $d$ columns, independent of $S$, have row span $U$ of dimension at most $T$. Suppose an output $\\widehat S$ is drawn from a probability kernel depending only on $(A,AS)$. Put $u=P_US$ and $v=(I-P_U)S$. For an arbitrary output in $\\mathbb R^d$ and $0<\\epsilon<1$, $$\\label{h:ev:direct-residual}\n \\Pr\\{\\|\\widehat S-S\\|\\le\\epsilon\\}\n \\le\\frac12+\\frac{T}{2d(1-\\epsilon^2)}.$$ More generally, for $0<b_0<1$ and $0\\le\\epsilon<\\sqrt{1-b_0}$, $$\\label{h:ev:antipodal}\n \\Pr\\{\\|\\widehat S-S\\|\\le\\epsilon\\}\n \\le\\frac12+\\frac12\\Pr\\{\\|u\\|^2\\ge b_0\\}\n \\le\\frac12+\\frac{T}{2b_0d}.$$ For a unit output one also has $$\\label{h:ev:reflection-expectation}\n \\mathbb E\\langle\\widehat S,v\\rangle=0,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E\\langle\\widehat S,S\\rangle\n \\le\\mathbb E\\|u\\|\\le\\sqrt{T/d}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-ev-reflection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge1$ be an integer and $T\\ge0$. Let $S$ be uniform on $S^{d-1}$. Let a matrix $A$ with $d$ columns, independent of $S$, have row span $U$ of dimension at most $T$. Suppose an output $\\widehat S$ is drawn from a probability kernel depending only on $(A,AS)$. Put $u=P_US$ and $v=(I-P_U)S$. For an arbitrary output in $\\mathbb R^d$ and $0<\\epsilon<1$, $$\\label{h:ev:direct-residual}\n \\Pr\\{\\|\\widehat S-S\\|\\le\\epsilon\\}\n \\le\\frac12+\\frac{T}{2d(1-\\epsilon^2)}.$$ More generally, for $0<b_0<1$ and $0\\le\\epsilon<\\sqrt{1-b_0}$, $$\\label{h:ev:antipodal}\n \\Pr\\{\\|\\widehat S-S\\|\\le\\epsilon\\}\n \\le\\frac12+\\frac12\\Pr\\{\\|u\\|^2\\ge b_0\\}\n \\le\\frac12+\\frac{T}{2b_0d}.$$ For a unit output one also has $$\\label{h:ev:reflection-expectation}\n \\mathbb E\\langle\\widehat S,v\\rangle=0,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E\\langle\\widehat S,S\\rangle\n \\le\\mathbb E\\|u\\|\\le\\sqrt{T/d}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $d\\ge1$ be an integer and $T\\ge0$. Let $S$ be uniform on $S^{d-1}$. Let a matrix $A$ with $d$ columns, independent of $S$, have row span $U$ of dimension at most $T$. For an arbitrary output in $\\mathbb R^d$ and $0<\\epsilon<1$, $$\\label{h:ev:direct-residual}\n \\Pr\\{\\|\\widehat S-S\\|\\le\\epsilon\\}\n \\le\\frac12+\\frac{T}{2d(1-\\epsilon^2)}.$$ More generally, for $0<b_0<1$ and $0\\le\\epsilon<\\sqrt{1-b_0}$, $$\\label{h:ev:antipodal}\n \\Pr\\{\\|\\widehat S-S\\|\\le\\epsilon\\}\n \\le\\frac12+\\frac12\\Pr\\{\\|u\\|^2\\ge b_0\\}\n \\le\\frac12+\\frac{T}{2b_0d}.$$ For a unit output one also has $$\\label{h:ev:reflection-expectation}\n \\mathbb E\\langle\\widehat S,v\\rangle=0,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E\\langle\\widehat S,S\\rangle\n \\le\\mathbb E\\|u\\|\\le\\sqrt{T/d}.$$",
      "explanation": "Reflection across the observed row span bounds full-data estimation success and correlation with the residual signal.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-alt-selection",
        "exact_quote": "For measurable routes $g_j\\ge0$ with $\\sum_jg_j\\le1$, $$\\label{h:alt:selection-bound}\n \\sum_{j=1}^N\\int g_j\\,d\\eta_j\n \\le N^{1/p}U\\,\\lambda(\\mathcal D)^{1-1/p}.$$ A sufficient condition for <a href=\"#h:alt:test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:alt:test\">[h:alt:test]</a> is $\\eta_j=f_j\\lambda$, where $f_j\\ge0$ vanishes outside $\\mathcal D$ and $\\|f_j\\|_{L^p(\\lambda)}\\le U$.*",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-alt-selection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(E,\\mathcal E,\\lambda)$ be a measure space, let $\\mathcal D\\in\\mathcal E$ have finite measure, let $p>1$, let $U\\ge0$, and let $N\\ge1$ be an integer. Suppose finite nonnegative measures $\\eta_1,\\ldots,\\eta_N$ are supported on $\\mathcal D$, and that for every measurable $0\\le g\\le1$, $$\\label{h:alt:test}\n \\int g\\,d\\eta_j\n \\le U\\left(\\int_{\\mathcal D}g\\,d\\lambda\\right)^{1-1/p}\n \\qquad(1\\le j\\le N).$$ For measurable routes $g_j\\ge0$ with $\\sum_jg_j\\le1$, $$\\label{h:alt:selection-bound}\n \\sum_{j=1}^N\\int g_j\\,d\\eta_j\n \\le N^{1/p}U\\,\\lambda(\\mathcal D)^{1-1/p}.$$ A sufficient condition for <a href=\"#h:alt:test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:alt:test\">[h:alt:test]</a> is $\\eta_j=f_j\\lambda$, where $f_j\\ge0$ vanishes outside $\\mathcal D$ and $\\|f_j\\|_{L^p(\\lambda)}\\le U$.*",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-alt-selection",
          "exact_quote": "*In particular, if $r>0$, $m,\\beta\\in\\mathbb R$, and positive constants $H,V,L$ satisfy $$U\\le HLr^{\\beta-m(1-1/p)},\\qquad\n \\lambda(\\mathcal D)\\le Vr^m,$$ then the right side of <a href=\"#h:alt:selection-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:alt:selection-bound\">[h:alt:selection-bound]</a> is at most $$\\label{h:alt:scale-cancellation}\n N^{1/p}H V^{1-1/p}Lr^\\beta.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(E,\\mathcal E,\\lambda)$ be a measure space, let $\\mathcal D\\in\\mathcal E$ have finite measure, let $p>1$, let $U\\ge0$, and let $N\\ge1$ be an integer. Suppose finite nonnegative measures $\\eta_1,\\ldots,\\eta_N$ are supported on $\\mathcal D$, and that for every measurable $0\\le g\\le1$, $$\\label{h:alt:test}\n \\int g\\,d\\eta_j\n \\le U\\left(\\int_{\\mathcal D}g\\,d\\lambda\\right)^{1-1/p}\n \\qquad(1\\le j\\le N).$$ For measurable routes $g_j\\ge0$ with $\\sum_jg_j\\le1$, $$\\label{h:alt:selection-bound}\n \\sum_{j=1}^N\\int g_j\\,d\\eta_j\n \\le N^{1/p}U\\,\\lambda(\\mathcal D)^{1-1/p}.$$ A sufficient condition for <a href=\"#h:alt:test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:alt:test\">[h:alt:test]</a> is $\\eta_j=f_j\\lambda$, where $f_j\\ge0$ vanishes outside $\\mathcal D$ and $\\|f_j\\|_{L^p(\\lambda)}\\le U$.*\n\n\n*In particular, if $r>0$, $m,\\beta\\in\\mathbb R$, and positive constants $H,V,L$ satisfy $$U\\le HLr^{\\beta-m(1-1/p)},\\qquad\n \\lambda(\\mathcal D)\\le Vr^m,$$ then the right side of <a href=\"#h:alt:selection-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:alt:selection-bound\">[h:alt:selection-bound]</a> is at most $$\\label{h:alt:scale-cancellation}\n N^{1/p}H V^{1-1/p}Lr^\\beta.$$",
      "explanation": "Selection among measures on a common finite-measure domain costs a root of the number of destinations.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-alt-selection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(E,\\mathcal E,\\lambda)$ be a measure space, let $\\mathcal D\\in\\mathcal E$ have finite measure, let $p>1$, let $U\\ge0$, and let $N\\ge1$ be an integer. Suppose finite nonnegative measures $\\eta_1,\\ldots,\\eta_N$ are supported on $\\mathcal D$, and that for every measurable $0\\le g\\le1$, $$\\label{h:alt:test}\n \\int g\\,d\\eta_j\n \\le U\\left(\\int_{\\mathcal D}g\\,d\\lambda\\right)^{1-1/p}\n \\qquad(1\\le j\\le N).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-density",
        "exact_quote": "There is a universal $C_2\\ge1$ such that $\\eta_{f,z,r}=h_{f,z,r}\\lambda_k$ for a nonnegative density with $$\\label{h:cube:density-bound}\n \\|h_{f,z,r}\\|_{L^p(\\lambda_k)}\n \\le C_2^n Lr^{\\beta-k(1-1/p)}.$$ If $\\mathcal B(z,r)\\ne\\varnothing$, fix any $u_*\\in\\mathcal B(z,r)$ and set",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-density",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be an integer with $1\\le k\\le p$, and let a Borel function $f:Q\\to[0,1]$ satisfy <a href=\"#h:cube:local\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:cube:local\">[h:cube:local]</a> with $L\\ge0$. For $z\\in\\mathbb R^n$ and $r>0$, define the finite joint measure $$\\label{h:cube:joint-measure}\n \\eta_{f,z,r}(E)\n =\\int\\!\\int_{\\mathcal B(z,r)}\n       \\mathbf 1_E(A,A\\iota(u))f(u)\\,du\\,\\gamma_k(dA)$$ for Borel $E\\subseteq\\mathbb R^{k\\times d}\\times\\mathbb R^k$. There is a universal $C_2\\ge1$ such that $\\eta_{f,z,r}=h_{f,z,r}\\lambda_k$ for a nonnegative density with $$\\label{h:cube:density-bound}\n \\|h_{f,z,r}\\|_{L^p(\\lambda_k)}\n \\le C_2^n Lr^{\\beta-k(1-1/p)}.$$ If $\\mathcal B(z,r)\\ne\\varnothing$, fix any $u_*\\in\\mathcal B(z,r)$ and set",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-density",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{h:cube:ellipsoid-support}\n \\mathcal E_{u_*,r}\n =\\{(A,y):y\\in A\\iota(u_*)+A B_d(0,4r)\\}.$$ Every such density, for this same $z,r,u_*$, vanishes almost everywhere outside $\\mathcal E_{u_*,r}$, and",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-density",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{h:cube:ellipsoid-volume}\n \\lambda_k(\\mathcal E_{u_*,r})\\le C_2^n r^k.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $k$ be an integer with $1\\le k\\le p$, and let a Borel function $f:Q\\to[0,1]$ satisfy <a href=\"#h:cube:local\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:cube:local\">[h:cube:local]</a> with $L\\ge0$. For $z\\in\\mathbb R^n$ and $r>0$, define the finite joint measure $$\\label{h:cube:joint-measure}\n \\eta_{f,z,r}(E)\n =\\int\\!\\int_{\\mathcal B(z,r)}\n       \\mathbf 1_E(A,A\\iota(u))f(u)\\,du\\,\\gamma_k(dA)$$ for Borel $E\\subseteq\\mathbb R^{k\\times d}\\times\\mathbb R^k$. There is a universal $C_2\\ge1$ such that $\\eta_{f,z,r}=h_{f,z,r}\\lambda_k$ for a nonnegative density with $$\\label{h:cube:density-bound}\n \\|h_{f,z,r}\\|_{L^p(\\lambda_k)}\n \\le C_2^n Lr^{\\beta-k(1-1/p)}.$$ If $\\mathcal B(z,r)\\ne\\varnothing$, fix any $u_*\\in\\mathcal B(z,r)$ and set $$\\label{h:cube:ellipsoid-support}\n \\mathcal E_{u_*,r}\n =\\{(A,y):y\\in A\\iota(u_*)+A B_d(0,4r)\\}.$$ Every such density, for this same $z,r,u_*$, vanishes almost everywhere outside $\\mathcal E_{u_*,r}$, and $$\\label{h:cube:ellipsoid-volume}\n \\lambda_k(\\mathcal E_{u_*,r})\\le C_2^n r^k.$$",
      "explanation": "Local cube mass transfers to a joint exact-label density on a common projected ellipsoid.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-density",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be an integer with $1\\le k\\le p$, and let a Borel function $f:Q\\to[0,1]$ satisfy <a href=\"#h:cube:local\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:cube:local\">[h:cube:local]</a> with $L\\ge0$. For $z\\in\\mathbb R^n$ and $r>0$, define the finite joint measure $$\\label{h:cube:joint-measure}\n \\eta_{f,z,r}(E)\n =\\int\\!\\int_{\\mathcal B(z,r)}\n       \\mathbf 1_E(A,A\\iota(u))f(u)\\,du\\,\\gamma_k(dA)$$ for Borel $E\\subseteq\\mathbb R^{k\\times d}\\times\\mathbb R^k$.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-block",

        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-block",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k,N$ be integers with $1\\le k\\le p$ and $N\\ge1$, and let Borel $f_1,\\ldots,f_N:Q\\to[0,1]$ satisfy <a href=\"#h:cube:local\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:cube:local\">[h:cube:local]</a> with the same $L\\ge0$.           "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-success",
        "exact_quote": "There are universal constants $D\\ge e$, $d_0\\ge16$, and $c>0$ with the following property. Let $d\\ge d_0$, $M\\ge0$, and $T\\ge0$ be integers, with $M\\le d^2$ and $0<\\epsilon\\le1/10$. Every learner in Definition <a href=\"#h:model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:model\">1</a> with these memory and sample bounds has angular success probability under $s=\\iota(u)$, $u\\sim du$, at most $$\\label{h:cube:success-bound}\n \\min\\left\\{1,\\,\n       D^{n(m+1)}\\epsilon^{n/4}\\right\\},\n \\qquad\n m=\\left\\lceil\\frac{T}{k_0}\\right\\rceil,\\qquad\n k_0=\\left\\lfloor\\frac n4\\right\\rfloor.$$ The probability averages over $u$, the fresh Gaussian samples, and the learner’s randomness. If that same learner has angular success probability at least $2/3$ for every $s\\in S^{d-1}$, then $$\\label{h:cube:sample-result}\n T\\ge c\\,d\\log(1/\\epsilon).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },

If that same learner has angular success probability at least $2/3$ for every $s\\in S^{d-1}$, then $$\\label{h:cube:sample-result}\n T\\ge c\\,d\\log(1/\\epsilon).$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "$$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{\\mathcal B(z,r)}f(u)\n   \\mathbf1_{\\{\\operatorname{dist}(u,V)\\le\\sqrt n\\,tr\\}}\\,du\n &\\le C_1^n Lr^\\beta t^{\\beta-j},\n \\label{h:cube:tube}\\\\\n \\int_{\\mathcal B(z,r)}f(u)\n   \\left(\\frac{\\operatorname{dist}(u,V)}{\\sqrt n}\\right)^{-k}\\,du\n &\\le C_1^n Lr^{\\beta-k}.\n \\label{h:cube:inverse-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The second integrand is nonnegative and extended-valued on $V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-cube-inverse",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:Q\\to[0,1]$ be Borel and satisfy <a href=\"#h:cube:local\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:cube:local\">[h:cube:local]</a> with $L\\ge0$.       "exact_statement": "Let $f:Q\\to[0,1]$ be Borel and satisfy <a href=\"#h:cube:local\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:cube:local\">[h:cube:local]</a> with $L\\ge0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-measurable-test",
        "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href=\"#h:dv:ambient-gram\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:ambient-gram\">7</a>, the exact image measure satisfies $$\\eta_\\mu(E)\\le \\mathcal B\\,\\lambda_m(E)^{1-1/p}\n \\label{h:dv:set-bound}$$ for every Borel set $E$ of finite $\\lambda_m$-measure. In particular $\\eta_\\mu\\ll\\lambda_m$, and the same inequality holds on the $\\lambda_m$-completion after extending $\\eta_\\mu$ to that completion. For every $\\lambda_m$-completed-measurable $0\\le\\varphi\\le1$, $$\\int\\varphi\\,d\\eta_\\mu\n \\le\\mathcal B\n \\left(\\int_{\\mathcal E_{z,R}}\\varphi\\,d\\lambda_m\\right)^{1-1/p}.\n \\label{h:dv:test-bound}$$ An infinite integral on the right is allowed.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-measurable-test",
          "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href=\"#h:dv:ambient-gram\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:ambient-gram\">7</a>, the exact image measure satisfies $$\\eta_\\mu(E)\\le \\mathcal B\\,\\lambda_m(E)^{1-1/p}\n \\label{h:dv:set-bound}$$ for every Borel set $E$ of finite $\\lambda_m$-measure. In particular $\\eta_\\mu\\ll\\lambda_m$, and the same inequality holds on the $\\lambda_m$-completion after extending $\\eta_\\mu$ to that completion. For every $\\lambda_m$-completed-measurable $0\\le\\varphi\\le1$, $$\\int\\varphi\\,d\\eta_\\mu\n \\le\\mathcal B\n \\left(\\int_{\\mathcal E_{z,R}}\\varphi\\,d\\lambda_m\\right)^{1-1/p}.\n \\label{h:dv:test-bound}$$ An infinite integral on the right is allowed.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href=\"#h:dv:ambient-gram\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:ambient-gram\">7</a>, the exact image measure satisfies $$\\eta_\\mu(E)\\le \\mathcal B\\,\\lambda_m(E)^{1-1/p}\n \\label{h:dv:set-bound}$$ for every Borel set $E$ of finite $\\lambda_m$-measure. In particular $\\eta_\\mu\\ll\\lambda_m$, and the same inequality holds on the $\\lambda_m$-completion after extending $\\eta_\\mu$ to that completion.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-gaussian-ac",
        "exact_quote": "If $A:\\mathbb R^d\\to\\mathbb R^m$ has full row rank, then $A_\\#\\sigma_d$ is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure. The same holds for $A_\\#\\mu$ whenever $\\mu$ is a finite Borel measure with $\\mu\\ll\\sigma_d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-gaussian-ac",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$ and $1\\le m<d$. If $A:\\mathbb R^d\\to\\mathbb R^m$ has full row rank, then $A_\\#\\sigma_d$ is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure. The same holds for $A_\\#\\mu$ whenever $\\mu$ is a finite Borel measure with $\\mu\\ll\\sigma_d$.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $d\\ge2$ and $1\\le m<d$. If $A:\\mathbb R^d\\to\\mathbb R^m$ has full row rank, then $A_\\#\\sigma_d$ is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure. The same holds for $A_\\#\\mu$ whenever $\\mu$ is a finite Borel measure with $\\mu\\ll\\sigma_d$.",
      "explanation": "Every full-row-rank projection of spherical area, or a measure dominated by it, is absolutely continuous.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-joint-density",
        "exact_quote": "Then $\\eta_\\mu$ has a nonnegative jointly Borel density $F(A,y)$ with respect to $\\lambda_m$. For $\\gamma_m$-almost every $A$, $F(A,\\cdot)$ is a density of $A_\\#\\mu$.*\n\n*If $\\mu$ is supported on $B(z,R)$, the density can be chosen to vanish outside the Borel set $\\mathcal E_{z,R}\\subseteq\\mathcal S_{z,R}$ defined in <a href=\"#h:dv:support-sets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:support-sets\">[h:dv:support-sets]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-joint-density",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$, $1\\le m<d$, and let $\\mu$ be a finite nonnegative Borel measure with $\\mu\\ll\\sigma_d$. For $\\gamma_m$-almost every $A$, $F(A,\\cdot)$ is a density of $A_\\#\\mu$.*\n\n*If $\\mu$ is supported on $B(z,R)$, the density can be chosen to vanish outside the Borel set $\\mathcal E_{z,R}\\subseteq\\mathcal S_{z,R}$ defined in <a href=\"#h:dv:support-sets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:support-sets\">[h:dv:support-sets]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-joint-l1",
        "exact_quote": "$$\\|F_\\delta-F\\|_{L^1(\\lambda_m)}\\longrightarrow0\n \\quad\\hbox{as }\\delta\\downarrow0,\n \\qquad\n \\|F\\|_{L^p(\\lambda_m)}\\le\\mathcal B.\n \\label{h:dv:joint-l1-bound}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-pointwise-density",
        "exact_quote": "$$w_\\delta(r)\n =v_m r^m\\frac{r}{\\delta^2}\\phi_\\delta(r e_1),\n \\qquad r>0,\n \\label{h:dv:radial-mixture}$$ where $e_1$ is a unit coordinate vector. This is a probability density on $(0,\\infty)$, and $\\phi_\\delta$ is the corresponding mixture of uniform ball densities. Define $$F^\\sharp(A,y)=\n \\begin{cases}\n  \\displaystyle\\lim_{i\\to\\infty}F_{1/i}(A,y),\n       &\\text{if this limit exists and is finite},\\\\\n  0,   &\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{h:dv:pointwise-version}$$ Then $F^\\sharp$ is jointly Borel. For every full-row-rank $A$, $F^\\sharp(A,\\cdot)$ is a density of $A_\\#\\mu$ up to a Lebesgue null set, and it is a density of $\\eta_\\mu$ with respect to $\\lambda_m$. Moreover $$\\int(F^\\sharp)^p\\,d\\lambda_m\\le\\mathcal B^p.\n \\label{h:dv:pointwise-moment}$$",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-auto-corollary-1",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#h:dv:ambient-gram\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:ambient-gram\">7</a>, suppose that $$d\\ge16,\\qquad p=\\lfloor d/8\\rfloor,\\qquad\n 1\\le m\\le p,\\qquad \\frac{d-1}{2}\\le\\beta\\le\\frac d2,\n \\label{h:dv:parameters}$$ Then, for every $0\\le j\\le p-2$, $$\\beta-j-m\\ge\\frac{d-1}{2}-2p+2\n \\ge\\frac d4+\\frac32,\n \\qquad J_j\\le C^d,\n \\label{h:dv:dimension-margin}$$ where $C\\ge1$ is an absolute constant. Thus the right side of <a href=\"#h:dv:gram-product\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:gram-product\">[h:dv:gram-product]</a> is at most $C^{d(p-1)}H^pR^{\\beta p-m(p-1)}$, uniformly also for short blocks $m<p$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-auto-corollary-1",

          "source_id": "PM-140.1"

      "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#h:dv:ambient-gram\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:ambient-gram\">7</a>, suppose that $$d\\ge16,\\qquad p=\\lfloor d/8\\rfloor,\\qquad\n 1\\le m\\le p,\\qquad \\frac{d-1}{2}\\le\\beta\\le\\frac d2,\n \\label{h:dv:parameters}$$ Then, for every $0\\le j\\le p-2$, $$\\beta-j-m\\ge\\frac{d-1}{2}-2p+2\n \\ge\\frac d4+\\frac32,\n \\qquad J_j\\le C^d,\n \\label{h:dv:dimension-margin}$$ where $C\\ge1$ is an absolute constant.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-ev-density",
        "exact_quote": "Assume <a href=\"#h:dv:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:dv:parameters\">[h:dv:parameters]</a>, and let $H,R>0$ and $z\\in\\mathbb R^d$. Let $f:S^{d-1}\\to[0,1]$ be Borel measurable, let $\\nu=f\\sigma$, and suppose $\\nu(B(x,r))\\le H r^\\beta$ for every $x\\in\\mathbb R^d$ and $r>0$. For $\\mu=\\mathbf 1_{B(z,R)}\\nu$, the pushforward $A_\\#\\mu$ has, for almost every $A$, a Lebesgue density $F(A,\\cdot)$. There is an absolute $C_{\\mathrm D}\\ge1$ for which a jointly measurable nonnegative choice of these densities satisfies $$\\label{h:ev:density-moment}\n \\int F(A,y)^p\\,d\\lambda_m(A,y)\n \\le C_{\\mathrm D}^{d(p-1)}\n       H^pR^{\\beta p-m(p-1)}.$$ The density represents the exact labels $As$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-ev-density",

          "source_id": "PM-140.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-hemisphere-cap",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$, $n=d-1$, and let $\\sigma_d$ be uniform probability on $S^{d-1}$. Then $$\\sigma_d(B(z,r))\\le(4r)^n\n \\qquad(z\\in\\mathbb R^d,\\ r>0).\n \\label{h:dv:hemisphere-ball}$$ Let $\\epsilon>0$, let $\\kappa$ be any probability law on $S^{d-1}$, independent of the target, and put $h(s)=\\int\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\|s-u\\|\\le\\epsilon\\}}\\,\\kappa(du)$. For $0<\\beta<n$, every $z\\in\\mathbb R^d$, and every $r>0$, $$\\int_{B(z,r)}h\\,d\\sigma_d\n \\le4^n\\min\\{r^n,\\epsilon^n\\}\n \\le4^n\\epsilon^{n-\\beta}r^\\beta.\n \\label{h:dv:terminal-coefficient}$$ The same upper bound holds for the angular-success function, since angular distance at most $\\epsilon$ implies chordal distance at most $\\epsilon$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-dv-hemisphere-cap",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$, $n=d-1$, and let $\\sigma_d$ be uniform probability on $S^{d-1}$. Then $$\\sigma_d(B(z,r))\\le(4r)^n\n \\qquad(z\\in\\mathbb R^d,\\ r>0).\n \\label{h:dv:hemisphere-ball}$$ Let $\\epsilon>0$, let $\\kappa$ be any probability law on $S^{d-1}$, independent of the target, and put $h(s)=\\int\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\|s-u\\|\\le\\epsilon\\}}\\,\\kappa(du)$. For $0<\\beta<n$, every $z\\in\\mathbb R^d$, and every $r>0$, $$\\int_{B(z,r)}h\\,d\\sigma_d\n \\le4^n\\min\\{r^n,\\epsilon^n\\}\n \\le4^n\\epsilon^{n-\\beta}r^\\beta.\n \\label{h:dv:terminal-coefficient}$$ The same upper bound holds for the angular-success function, since angular distance at most $\\epsilon$ implies chordal distance at most $\\epsilon$.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $d\\ge2$, $n=d-1$, and let $\\sigma_d$ be uniform probability on $S^{d-1}$. Then $$\\sigma_d(B(z,r))\\le(4r)^n\n \\qquad(z\\in\\mathbb R^d,\\ r>0).\n \\label{h:dv:hemisphere-ball}$$ Let $\\epsilon>0$, let $\\kappa$ be any probability law on $S^{d-1}$, independent of the target, and put $h(s)=\\int\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\|s-u\\|\\le\\epsilon\\}}\\,\\kappa(du)$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-ev-polar-cap",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$, $n=d-1$, and let $\\sigma$ be uniform on $S^{d-1}$. There is an absolute $C_0\\ge1$ such that $\\sigma(B(z,R))\\le C_0^dR^n$ for every ambient center $z$ and every $R>0$. Let $\\epsilon>0$. If a unit output is drawn from a law independent of the signal, its success function $h(s)$, for either angular error at most $\\epsilon$ or chordal error at most $\\epsilon$, satisfies $$\\label{h:ev:polar-terminal}\n \\int_{B(z,R)}h\\,d\\sigma\n \\le C_0^d\\min(R^n,\\epsilon^n)\n \\le C_0^d\\epsilon^{n-\\beta}R^\\beta\n \\qquad(0<\\beta<n).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-ev-polar-cap",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$, $n=d-1$, and let $\\sigma$ be uniform on $S^{d-1}$. There is an absolute $C_0\\ge1$ such that $\\sigma(B(z,R))\\le C_0^dR^n$ for every ambient center $z$ and every $R>0$. Let $\\epsilon>0$. If a unit output is drawn from a law independent of the signal, its success function $h(s)$, for either angular error at most $\\epsilon$ or chordal error at most $\\epsilon$, satisfies $$\\label{h:ev:polar-terminal}\n \\int_{B(z,R)}h\\,d\\sigma\n \\le C_0^d\\min(R^n,\\epsilon^n)\n \\le C_0^d\\epsilon^{n-\\beta}R^\\beta\n \\qquad(0<\\beta<n).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $d\\ge2$, $n=d-1$, and let $\\sigma$ be uniform on $S^{d-1}$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Persistent state capacity** ([source](#stmt-h-model)): After each number of samples its persistent state belongs to a prescribed set of at most $2^M$ elements.
- **Terminal output rule** ([source](#stmt-h-model)): At a stopping index its unit output $\widehat s\in S^{d-1}$ is generated from the terminal state, the stopping index, and fresh randomness only.
- **Angular accuracy** ([source](#stmt-h-model)): Accuracy means $$\arccos\langle \widehat s,s\rangle\le\epsilon.$$
- **Spherical reference measure** ([source](#h:introduction)): Write $\sigma_d$ for uniform probability on $S^{d-1}$.
- **Local mass scale** ([source](#h:local:bound)): For $R>0$, say that a measurable $h:S^{d-1}\to[0,1]$ has local mass at scale $R$ if $$\label{h:local:bound} \int_{B(z,r)}h\,d\sigma\le(Rr)^a \qquad(z\in S^{d-1},\ r>0).$$
- **Main geometric parameters** ([source](#h:local:parameters)): For the main proof set $$\label{h:local:parameters} n=d-1,\qquad a=\frac n2,\qquad q=\left\lfloor\frac n8\right\rfloor.$$
- **Common exact support** ([source](#h:projection)): For a closed support ball $B(z,R)$, define $$\label{h:dv:support-sets} \mathcal E_{z,R}=\{(A,y):y\in Az+A B(0,R)\} \subseteq \mathcal S_{z,R} =\{(A,y):\\|y-Az\\|\le R\\|A\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}.$$
- **Fresh continuation success** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Starting from a specified state $u\in Q_t$ before its sample-free stopping check, define $h_{t,u}(s)$ to be the expected terminal score $\mathbb E[\ell(s,\widehat s)]$ with signal $s$ and fresh future samples and randomness.
- **Paired-coordinate sphere embedding** ([source](#pm-h-21)): Define $$\label{h:cube:map} \iota(u)=\frac1{\sqrt n} \bigl(u_1,\ldots,u_n,\sqrt{1-u_1^2},\ldots, \sqrt{1-u_n^2},0,\ldots,0\bigr)\in\mathbb R^d,$$ with $d-2n$ zero coordinates.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-memory | M | ≤ | $Ad^2$ | Fixed finite memory coefficient; sufficiently large dimension. |
| Q-accuracy | \epsilon | ≤ | $1/10$ | Positive accuracy in the stated range. |
| Q-success | Success probability | ≥ | $\frac23$ | Premise of the main lower bound. |
| Q-horizon | T | ≥ | $c_A\,d\log(1/\epsilon)$ | Fixed finite memory coefficient; the constant and dimension threshold are not numerically specified. |
| Q-state | Persistent state count | ≤ | $2^M$ | After every number of samples. |
| Q-local-exponent | a | = | $\frac n2$ | Main proof parameters. |
| Q-moment | q | = | $\left\lfloor\frac n8\right\rfloor$ | Main proof, including short blocks. |
| Q-destinations | N | ≤ | $(m+2)2^M$ | Fixed incoming state and index; local stopping offsets only. |
| Q-cap | \sigma_d(B(z,r)) | ≤ | $r^{d-1}$ | Sphere-centered ambient balls. |
| Q-reflection-endpoint | Success probability | ≤ | $\frac{62}{99}$ | The exact residual estimate is applied before averaging over the seed. |
| Q-linear-threshold | T | > | $d/4$ | Stipulated average success and accuracy range of the main theorem. |
| Q-cube-alpha | \alpha | = | $\frac n4$ | The cube parameters and local-mass convention. |
| Q-cube-beta | \beta | = | $\frac{3n}{4}$ | The cube parameters and local-mass convention. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R-local** [claim](#stmt-h-local-caps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-local-caps): Spherical cap geometry and integration over a signal-independent output law. -> Terminal local-mass scale.
- **R-gram** [claim](#stmt-h-dv-ambient-gram) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-dv-ambient-gram): Affine tube covering, inverse-distance averaging and successive orthogonalization. -> Inverse Gram determinant moment.
- **R-density** [claim](#stmt-h-dv-finite-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-proj-density-by-tests-2): Gaussian smoothing followed by compact-test duality. -> Exact joint density norm.
- **R-volume** [claim](#stmt-h-ev-ellipsoid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-ev-ellipsoid): Singular-value ellipsoid volume and Gaussian row norm bounds. -> Average support volume.
- **R-block** [claim](#stmt-h-block-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-block-bound): Localization and Hölder on data space and destinations. -> Backward local-mass update.
- **R-borel** [claim](#stmt-h-stream-borel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-stream-borel): Borel representatives and coupling outside a common null set. -> Prior-preserving Borel rules.
- **R-destinations** [claim](#stmt-h-stream-categories) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-stream-categories): Enumerate terminal local stopping offsets and continuing states. -> Local destination count.
- **R-stream** [claim](#stmt-h-stream-success) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-stream-success): Backward induction over consecutive blocks and original seed averaging. -> Finite-horizon success estimate.
- **R-reflection** [claim](#stmt-h-ev-reflection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-ev-reflection): Reflect the residual signal across the observed row span. -> Memory-independent constant-accuracy endpoint.
- **R-main** [claim](#stmt-h-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-ev-reflection-2): Logarithmic extraction with a separate reflection endpoint for bounded logarithms. -> Precision-dependent sample lower bound.
- **R-cube** [claim](#stmt-h-cube-success) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-cube-success): Transfer cube local mass to ambient growth and iterate common-support selection. -> Cube-average precision estimate and every-signal full-range consequence.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **stream-state**: Persistent information is limited to a finite state; terminal output cannot revisit discarded observations.
- **memory-precision**: Under the stated average-success premise, fixed quadratic retained memory forces a measurement horizon proportional to dimension times the logarithm of inverse angular accuracy.
- **terminal-local-mass**: A signal-independent terminal output law initializes the all-radius bound on successful mass at the requested angular accuracy.
- **backward-block**: Backward routing among continuations preserves the local radius exponent while increasing the successful-mass scale according to the destination count.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **real-register-memory**: Treat the real-register projection benchmark as a finite-bit streaming implementation. -> Stored real scalars do not establish bounded persistent bit capacity.
- **adaptive-revisiting**: Apply the streaming theorem to rows chosen by the learner or to repeated access to discarded observations. -> These observation and access rules leave the stated model.
- **growing-memory-coefficient**: Use the fixed-coefficient lower bound with an unchanged positive constant as the memory coefficient grows with dimension. -> The theorem permits dependence of its constants on the fixed memory coefficient.
- **individual-hardness**: Infer that every individual unit signal is hard for every learner. -> Averaging an every-signal success guarantee supplies the prior premise but does not assert individual hardness.
- **posterior-radius**: Interpret the propagated local-mass scale as the radius supporting a posterior distribution. -> The backward bound controls successful mass under the original prior rather than a normalized posterior.
- **cube-all-accuracy**: Infer the full accuracy-range lower bound from cube-average success alone. -> The cube argument uses an every-signal guarantee and a separate uniform-sphere endpoint for the remaining accuracies.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results bound success probabilities for continuous Gaussian observations and measurable finite-state learners rather than construct a finite-bit estimator. The displayed projection benchmark stores real scalars and supplies no finite-bit upper bound. A finite exact test of selected arithmetic consequences would not decide these distributional and asymptotic claims.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | A streaming estimator can use the lower bound to assess whether a claimed precision and observation horizon are compatible with its retained-state budget; the paper does not supply a software implementation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A game or simulation workload is not specified. The signal and observation experiment alone does not establish a simulation contract. |
| tools | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | A review of streaming-estimator resource claims can use the theorem as a conditional feasibility check. A numerical certification tool is not supplied, since the constants and dimension threshold are existential. |
| algorithms | True | strong | inspirational | inferred | The lower bound constrains all learners satisfying the finite-state model, including randomized rules and bounded stopping, rather than prescribing a recovery algorithm. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The state-space cardinality gives a representation constraint for a streaming estimator. It does not identify a particular data structure that attains the required precision. |
| hardware_accelerators | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The retained-bit constraint can inform memory budgeting for an accelerator running the same estimator model. Unrestricted computation makes this a conditional memory constraint, not a throughput or accelerator design result. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The model studies estimation success under a data-independent seed, not secrecy, adversarial security, or computational hardness. |
| machine_learning | True | strong | inspirational | inferred | The main theorem directly bounds the observation horizon for learning a unit signal to angular precision under finite persistent memory. Subquadratic memory gives an absolute leading constant after an eventual dimension threshold. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The fresh-row Kaczmarz update supplies a real-register projection benchmark for exact linear equations. Its expected error contraction explains the precision scale but does not supply a finite-bit implementation guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The stated resource is a learner’s total persistent state. The paper distinguishes a retained transcript from a per-message budget and supplies no distributed topology or communication contract. |
| other (geometric_measure_theory) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The exact Gaussian projection theorem gives a joint density bound for finite measures satisfying ambient ball growth. It is an analytic interface for localized successful mass, not a normalized posterior or an executable estimator. |
